## 1. A Photonic Integrated Long-distance Quantum Communication Network

- **Score**: 9.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Nature  |  **Feed**: Nature Photonics
- **Direction**: Quantum Networks
- **Published**: 2026-06-19
- **Authors**: Likang Zhang, Jiawei Pan, Teng-Yun Chen, Zheng-Ping Li, Yichen Lu, Wei Li, Wen-Xin Pan, Wei Ke, Yuyao Guo, Wenwen Li, Sheng-Kai Liao, Cheng-Zhi Peng, Linjie Zhou, Xinlun Cai, Feihu Xu, Jian-Wei Pan
- **Link**: [https://www.nature.com/articles/s41566-026-01944-w](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41566-026-01944-w)
- **Tags**: `quantum network`  `twin-field QKD`  `photonic integrated circuit`  `long-distance fiber`  `quantum key distribution`

### TL;DR

A photonic integrated quantum network using twin-field QKD over 540 km fiber with full connectivity between four users.

### Motivation

To demonstrate a scalable, long-distance quantum communication network that can support multiple users with high secret key rates.

### Method

The network uses a spine-leaf architecture with twin-field QKD protocol, photonic integrated circuits, and operates at 1.25 GHz clock rate.

### Result

Achieved secret key rate of 9.38 × 10⁻⁹ bit per pulse over up to 540 km fiber with full connectivity between any two user pairs.

### Conclusion

This work shows the feasibility of integrated photonic quantum networks for practical long-distance quantum communication.

*Keywords: quantum network, twin-field QKD, photonic integrated circuit, long-distance fiber, quantum key distribution*

### TL;DR

This paper derives fundamental bounds on work extraction from finite-time closed quantum dynamics, revealing a trade-off between power and efficiency.

### Motivation

Understanding the fundamental limits of work extraction in quantum systems is crucial for designing efficient quantum engines and protocols, especially when time constraints are present.

### Method

The authors use optimal control theory and quantum thermodynamic principles to analyze work extraction from closed quantum systems undergoing finite-time unitary evolution.

### Result

They establish a trade-off relation between the extracted work and the operation time, showing that faster protocols incur higher dissipation and lower efficiency.

### Conclusion

The results provide fundamental bounds that guide the design of near-optimal work extraction protocols in quantum thermodynamics.

- **Tags**: `quantum repeaters`  `entanglement distribution`  `first-generation repeaters`  `analytical model`  `quantum networks`

### TL;DR

Analytical model for entanglement distribution time in first-generation quantum repeater chains.

### Motivation

First-generation quantum repeaters rely on heralded entanglement generation and purification, but their performance is often evaluated via simulation. An analytical formula for entanglement distribution time enables rapid optimization and comparison.

### Method

Derives closed-form expressions for the expected time to distribute a Bell pair over a chain of N segments, accounting for probabilistic entanglement generation, swapping, and purification steps.

### Result

Provides exact analytical results for the distribution time as a function of segment length, memory coherence time, and purification efficiency, validated against Monte Carlo simulations.

### Conclusion

The analytical model offers a practical tool for designing and optimizing first-generation quantum repeater chains, highlighting trade-offs between segment length and memory quality.

*Keywords: quantum repeaters, entanglement distribution, first-generation repeaters, analytical model, quantum networks*

## 6. Universal Thermodynamic Uncertainty Relation For Quantum $f$ Divergences

- **Source**: APS  |  **Feed**: Physical Review E
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-06-23
- **Authors**: Domingos S. P. Salazar
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/n2bt-jcm6](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/n2bt-jcm6)
- **Tags**: `thermodynamic uncertainty relation`  `quantum f-divergence`  `Petz divergence`  `quantum Fisher information`  `quantum thermodynamics`

### TL;DR

Establishes a universal thermodynamic uncertainty relation for quantum f-divergences, linking distinguishability to thermodynamic cost.

### Motivation

To derive a general bound on the distinguishability of quantum states in terms of thermodynamic quantities, extending classical thermodynamic uncertainty relations to the quantum regime.

### Method

Uses Petz f-divergences and operator convex functions to represent distinguishability as a mixture of quadratic contrasts, then applies the quantum Cramér-Rao bound.

### Result

A universal inequality relating any Petz f-divergence to the quantum Fisher information and the variance of an observable, providing a thermodynamic uncertainty relation for quantum processes.

### Conclusion

The result offers a unified framework for thermodynamic precision bounds in quantum systems, with implications for quantum metrology and thermodynamics.

*Keywords: thermodynamic uncertainty relation, quantum f-divergence, Petz divergence, quantum Fisher information, quantum thermodynamics*

### TL;DR

Tight bounds on the query complexity of quantum phase estimation with advice, including nearly matching lower bounds and a resolution of an open problem in unitary recurrence time.

### Motivation

Phase estimation is a fundamental subroutine, but its cost with limited advice (e.g., copies of an eigenstate or a state with known overlap) was not fully characterized. Understanding these trade-offs is crucial for algorithm design.

### Method

The authors develop algorithms and prove nearly matching lower bounds using a variant of the polynomial method with trigonometric polynomials, along with reductions to the unitary recurrence time problem.

### Result

They show that a small number of advice copies are not significantly better than no advice, and that many applications of an advice-preparing unitary do not reduce cost much. They also prove that error-probability reduction costs a factor of 1/ε, unlike search problems.

### Conclusion

### TL;DR

Proposes a qudit CHSH inequality whose maximal violation is achieved by stabilizer states with maximal Wigner negativity, linking nonlocality to Wigner negativity.

### Motivation

Generalizing Bell inequalities to higher-dimensional systems is challenging because qubit results do not directly extend; this work aims to connect nonlocality to Wigner negativity, a known necessary resource for qudit nonlocality.

### Method

Introduces a new CHSH-like inequality for qudits using correlations derived from the adjoint action of a generalized π/8 gate on stabilizer states, and analyzes the Bell operator in terms of Wigner negativity and singlet fraction.

### Result

The maximal violation is attained by a specific stabilizer state with maximal Wigner negativity; the Bell operator quantifies both singlet fraction and volume of Wigner negativity; bipartite entangled qudit states with Wigner negativity witness contextuality.

### Conclusion

Wigner negativity is a key resource for nonlocality in qudit systems, and the proposed inequality provides a direct operational link between nonlocality and Wigner negativity.

### Motivation

Entanglement Hamiltonians encode the entanglement structure of quantum many-body systems, but their experimental extraction is challenging. Variational methods offer a practical route, yet their convergence properties and reliability are not well understood.

The authors interpret the cost functional as an integral, enabling iterative quadrature schemes to reduce measurements. They also introduce a modified ansatz to capture deviations from the Bisognano-Wichmann form in lattice models.

The proposed methods reduce required measurements by over an order of magnitude even with noise, improve convergence and trainability, and provide a cost-function-level diagnostic for quantum phase transitions. However, low cost does not guarantee convergence in trace distance.

The authors define the symplectic rank based on the minimal number of Gaussian unitaries required to prepare a state from a Gaussian state, and relate it to the state's covariance matrix and Wigner function negativity.

The authors numerically compute 2n-point out-of-time-order correlators in the kicked top model and apply large deviation theory to define and analyze the time scale for freeness.

### Result

### TL;DR

The paper clarifies differences between quantum reference frame frameworks by linking them to the accessibility of the global charge, and shows that internal observers can measure the global charge via interference and classical communication.

### Motivation

Quantum reference frames are studied in different communities with non-equivalent frameworks; the paper aims to clarify the mathematical and physical distinctions, particularly regarding symmetry constraints and charge accessibility.

### Method

The authors compare weak and strong symmetry constraints, define perspectives based on operational capacities, and analyze existing arguments. They introduce a simple operational scenario with two physical postulates to demonstrate charge measurement.

### Result

The weak approach induces ambiguity in momenta and prevents reversible QRF transformations. Internal observers can measure the global charge by performing a relativized interference measurement and classical communication.

### Conclusion

The choice of symmetry constraint corresponds to whether the global charge is accessible; the weak approach is less restrictive but has limitations, while the strong approach allows reversible transformations.

- **Tags**: `noise`  `control`  `scattering`  `non-hermitian`  `quantum Fisher information`

### TL;DR

This paper derives fundamental bounds on non-Hermitian sensing using quantum Fisher information, showing that exceptional points can enhance sensitivity but are limited by decay rates and local density of states.

### Motivation

Exceptional points are promising for sensing due to enhanced spectral response, but whether they provide a genuine quantum advantage is debated. The authors aim to clarify this using a quantum Fisher information framework.

### Method

The authors use a scattering-matrix formalism to evaluate quantum Fisher information directly from scattering data, analyzing nondegenerate and degenerate poles including exceptional points of arbitrary order.

### Result

The quantum Fisher information per photon is bounded by decay rate, non-normality, and mode matching; exceptional points can enhance QFI compared to isolated modes, but moving away from the EP can further increase QFI by reducing decay rates.

### Conclusion

Exceptional points offer a genuine but limited advantage in quantum sensing, and the framework provides a unified perspective for designing quantum-limited non-Hermitian sensors.

*Keywords: quantum Fisher information, exceptional points, non-Hermitian sensing, scattering matrix, quantum metrology*

### Motivation

Understanding the emergence of thermodynamic behavior from microscopic dynamics is a fundamental question in statistical mechanics, and dynamical typicality provides a rigorous framework for this.

### Method

The authors consider deterministic classical lattice systems with continuous dynamical variables and sample initial conditions from a product distribution. They then analyze the temporal evolution of macroscopic observables using mathematical techniques from dynamical systems and probability theory.

### Result

They show that for any macroscopic observable, its time evolution is typical (i.e., close to the ensemble average) for most initial conditions, with deviations that vanish in the thermodynamic limit.

### Conclusion

### TL;DR

This paper reports the discovery of robust orbital-selective flat bands in layered transition-metal oxyhalides at room temperature using ARPES.

### Motivation

Flat bands can host strongly correlated electron states, but they are often fragile or require low temperatures; this work aims to find a stable room-temperature platform.

### Method

Angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES) was used to measure the electronic band structure of layered transition-metal oxyhalides.

### Result

Intrinsic flat bands were observed that remain stable at room temperature, with orbital selectivity.

### Conclusion

These materials provide a tunable platform for investigating strongly correlated electron states at ambient conditions.

### TL;DR

This paper uses magneto-optical spectroscopy to probe quantum anomalous Hall states in twisted bilayer WSe2, demonstrating electric-field control of magnetic order.

### Motivation

To investigate the quantum anomalous Hall phase in twisted bilayer WSe2 and understand the interplay between topology and magnetism in moiré materials.

### Method

Magneto-optical spectroscopy, specifically attractive polaron spectroscopy, is used to probe the electronic structure and magnetic ordering of twisted bilayer WSe2 under applied electric fields.

### Result

The experiments reveal a spontaneous quantum anomalous Hall phase and show that an electric field can switch between ferromagnetic and antiferromagnetic orders.

### Conclusion

Twisted bilayer WSe2 is a promising platform for studying topological phases with tunable magnetism, relevant for future quantum devices.

*Keywords: quantum anomalous Hall, twisted bilayer WSe2, magneto-optical spectroscopy, moiré materials, electric-field control*

Classical field simulation of vortex lattice melting in a rotating 2D Bose gas, focusing on finite-size effects and the KTHNY melting scenario.

### Motivation

To numerically study the thermal melting of a vortex lattice in a rotating Bose gas, complementing recent experiments and clarifying the role of finite-size effects.

### Method

Uses the stochastic projected Gross–Pitaevskii equation in a harmonic plus quartic trap to simulate finite-temperature equilibrium states, extracting vortex configurations from density snapshots.

### Result

Clear signatures of two-step KTHNY melting are observed; finite-size effects significantly affect defect proliferation at lower rotation frequencies.

### Conclusion

The classical field approach successfully reproduces the melting scenario and highlights the importance of finite-size effects in interpreting experiments.

- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7c28](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7c28)
- **Tags**: `dynamics`  `ultrafast`  `simulation`  `bandwidth`  `argon`

### TL;DR

This paper studies ultrafast photoionization dynamics in argon, focusing on causality and Wigner delays at the Amusia-Cooper minimum.

### Motivation

To understand the role of causality in photoionization when attosecond pulses are shorter than the Wigner delay, and to resolve gauge-dependent discrepancies in ionization dynamics.

### Method

Ab initio simulations using relativistic time-dependent configuration-interaction singles (RTDCIS) method in length and velocity gauges, combined with a simple analytical model and wave packet simulations.

### Result

Length gauge yields positive Wigner delays while velocity gauge yields negative delays of several femtoseconds, but both gauges produce causal electron behavior; weighted delays reconcile the model with simulations.

### Conclusion

The work provides deeper insight into ultrafast photoionization and causality in strongly correlated systems, but is unrelated to the user's research interests.

*Keywords: photoionization, Wigner delay, causality, attosecond physics, time-dependent configuration interaction*

- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7ca5](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7ca5)
- **Tags**: `control`  `free-energy`  `first-principles`  `adamantane clusters`  `nucleation`

### TL;DR

This paper studies the nucleation of adamantane clusters in weakly ionized plasmas using a free-energy framework, focusing on dispersion interactions and charging effects.

### Motivation

To understand the role of non-chemical interactions in determining cluster sizes in low-temperature plasmas, which is poorly understood.

### Method

Develops a unified free-energy model combining first-principles polarisability calculations with a dielectric plasma description, including collisional damping and screening.

### Result

The free-energy landscape shows a nucleation barrier with a critical radius; no effective equilibrium radius exists; cluster sizes are governed by kinetic balance.

### Conclusion

Plasma conditions (pressure, electron temperature, gas composition) can control nucleation scale, but steady cluster sizes arise from kinetics, not free-energy minimization.

*Keywords: adamantane clusters, nucleation, weakly ionized plasma, dispersion interactions, free-energy framework*

## 497. Orthogonal Phase Modulation And Lissajous Mode Decoupling In Light-induced Thermoelastic Spectroscopy For Real-time Multi-component Gas Sensing

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Other  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-18
- **Authors**: Hanxu Ma, Shunda Qiao, Ying He, Haiyue Sun And Yufei Ma
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3)
- **Tags**: `noise`  `spectroscopy`  `multiplexing`  `orthogonal phase modulation`  `light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a novel optical gas sensing technique using orthogonal phase modulation and Lissajous mode decoupling in light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy for real-time multi-component gas detection.

### Motivation

Real-time multi-component gas sensing is important for environmental monitoring and industrial safety, but existing methods suffer from channel crosstalk and complexity.

### Method

Two lasers are modulated with same frequency but 45° phase difference, co-excite a quartz tuning fork, and the piezoelectric signal is orthogonally demodulated to retrieve two gas concentrations independently.

### Result

The sensor achieves linear responses for CH4 and C2H2 with low systematic errors (0.54% and 0.79%) and detection limits of 0.32 ppm and 0.29 ppm, respectively.

### Conclusion

The proposed OPM-LITES sensor provides a simple, efficient solution for real-time multi-component gas detection with a new theoretical framework for mode decoupling.

*Keywords: orthogonal phase modulation, light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy, quartz tuning fork, multi-component gas sensing, mode decoupling*

This paper explains multidecadal preindustrial methane variability as arising from random noise in the source-sink imbalance, rather than slow climate changes or human activity.

### Motivation

Understanding the causes of preindustrial methane variability is important for interpreting ice core records and constraining natural methane cycles.

### Method

The authors use statistical modeling and ice core methane data to show that fast, random fluctuations in the methane source-sink balance can produce observed multidecadal variability.

### Result

They find that noise in the source-sink imbalance is sufficient to explain the observed variability, without invoking slow climate forcings.

### Conclusion

Preindustrial methane variability may be largely driven by stochastic processes, simplifying the interpretation of past methane dynamics.

### TL;DR

This paper studies the role of Drosophila Myc in mitigating mitochondrial and muscle defects caused by Metaxin-2 deficiency, a topic in developmental biology and mitochondrial disease.

### Motivation

Mutations in Metaxin-2 are linked to a progeria disorder in children, but the cellular mechanisms are poorly understood. The study aims to understand how Myc interacts with Mtx2 to maintain mitochondrial homeostasis and muscle maturation.

### Method

Using Drosophila as a model, the authors generated Mtx2-deficient flies and analyzed the effects on mitochondrial morphology, muscle structure, and gene expression. They then overexpressed Myc to test for rescue of the defects.

### Result

Myc overexpression ameliorates mitochondrial fragmentation and muscle maturation defects caused by Mtx2 deficiency, suggesting a functional interaction between Myc and Mtx2 pathways.

### Conclusion

Myc plays a protective role in mitochondrial homeostasis and muscle development, offering potential therapeutic targets for mitochondrial disorders.

- **Link**: [https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2534029123?af=R](https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2534029123?af=R)
- **Tags**: `DCTPP1`  `nucleotide pyrophosphatase`  `DNMT inhibitors`  `cancer therapy`  `structural biology`

### TL;DR

This paper studies the structural biology of DCTPP1 antagonists and their synergy with DNMT inhibitors for cancer therapy.

### Motivation

DCTPP1 is a nucleotide pyrophosphatase that prevents incorporation of modified nucleotides into DNA; inhibiting it may enhance the efficacy of DNMT inhibitors in cancer treatment.

### Method

The authors use X-ray crystallography to determine the structure of DCTPP1 with antagonists, and perform cellular assays to evaluate synergistic effects with DNMT inhibitors.

### Result

They identify potent DCTPP1 antagonists and show that combining them with DNMT inhibitors leads to enhanced cancer cell death.

### Conclusion

DCTPP1 is a viable therapeutic target, and its inhibition synergizes with DNMT inhibitors, offering a potential combination therapy for cancer.

*Keywords: DCTPP1, nucleotide pyrophosphatase, DNMT inhibitors, cancer therapy, structural biology*

To test the assumption that AI agents behave rationally or human-like under uncertainty.

- **Tags**: `computation`  `protein topology`  `Solomon link`  `molecular knot`  `protein design`

### TL;DR

This paper reports the computational design and cellular synthesis of two protein topological isomers: a Solomon link and a three-twist knot.

### Motivation

To demonstrate that complex protein topologies beyond simple knots can be designed and expressed in cells, advancing the field of protein engineering.

### Method

Computational design of protein sequences that fold into specific entangled topologies, followed by expression in E. coli and structural characterization.

### Result

Successful synthesis of both a Solomon link (two interlinked rings) and a three-twist knot in proteins, confirmed by structural analysis.

### Conclusion

Complex protein topologies can be rationally designed and produced in living cells, opening new possibilities for biomolecular materials and synthetic biology.

*Keywords: protein topology, Solomon link, molecular knot, protein design, cellular synthesis*

- **Tags**: `gan`  `microtubule`  `lattice parameters`  `ligand binding`  `structural biology`

### TL;DR

This paper studies microtubule lattice parameters and their regulation by ligand binding to stabilizing sites, using structural biology techniques.

### Motivation

Microtubules are key cytoskeletal components whose lattice geometry affects motor protein and MAP interactions; understanding how drugs modulate this lattice is important for cell biology and drug design.

### Method

The authors used X-ray crystallography, cryo-EM, and other structural methods to analyze microtubule lattice parameters in the presence of stabilizing ligands.

### Result

They identified specific lattice parameter changes induced by different ligands and mapped binding sites.

### Conclusion

Ligand binding to microtubule-stabilizing sites can tune lattice geometry, which may inform the design of better microtubule-targeting drugs.

*Keywords: microtubule, lattice parameters, ligand binding, structural biology, taxanes*

### TL;DR

This paper presents a spatiotemporal transcriptome atlas of ovule development in gymnosperms, focusing on plant developmental biology.

### Motivation

To understand the molecular and cellular mechanisms underlying ovule development in gymnosperms, which is key to elucidating the evolutionary origin of ovules.

### Method

Spatial transcriptomics and single-cell RNA sequencing were used to profile gene expression across different stages and regions of ovule development in a gymnosperm species.

### Result

The study identified dynamic gene expression patterns and key regulatory pathways involved in ovule initiation, differentiation, and maturation.

### Conclusion

The findings provide a comprehensive molecular framework for ovule development in gymnosperms and offer insights into the evolution of seed plants.

- **Link**: [https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2606703123?af=R](https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2606703123?af=R)
- **Tags**: `Duchenne muscular dystrophy`  `dystrophin`  `muscle regeneration`  `satellite cells`  `mouse model`

### TL;DR

This paper studies muscle weakness and regeneration in DMD-null mice, a model for Duchenne muscular dystrophy.

### Motivation

To understand the role of dystrophin isoforms in muscle function and regeneration, as existing mdx mice have a mild phenotype.

### Method

Phenotypic analysis of DMD-null mice, including muscle strength tests, regeneration assays, and satellite cell function studies.

### Result

DMD-null mice show severe muscle weakness, impaired regeneration, and deficient satellite cell function.

### Conclusion

DMD-null mice more accurately model human DMD and highlight the importance of dystrophin isoforms in muscle health.

*Keywords: Duchenne muscular dystrophy, dystrophin, muscle regeneration, satellite cells, mouse model*
